- **ajian-adopt**: Use to bring an existing project onto the ajian pipeline when ajian cannot read it yet. - **ajian-build**: Use to execute an approved implementation plan. Triggers include "/ajian-build NN", "build work order N", "execute the plan", "implement this". Runs the whole plan in ONE fresh subagent, tests and commits per task, ticks the plan's committed checkboxes as its ledger, and resumes cleanly after interruption. It does not review or merge — that is ajian-review. ([pripanggalih/ajian](https://github.com/pripanggalih/ajian/tree/HEAD/skills/build) · ★1)
- **ajian-design**: Use to build the visual world for a UI work order, by handing it to impeccable. Triggers include "/ajian-design NN", "design work order N", "build the UI for this feature", "seed the visual world". Runs between ajian-grill and ajian-plan: derives PRODUCT.md as a projection of the blueprint (not a fresh interview), then invokes impeccable's new-work flow to invent and build the surface and record DESIGN.md. Only for work orders that have UI. Requires impeccable (`npx impeccable install`) — without it this skill stops at its gate; it never designs the surface itself. ([pripanggalih/ajian](https://github.com/pripanggalih/ajian/tree/HEAD/skills/design) · ★1)
- **ajian-grill**: Use before building a work order, to sharpen it from brief to buildable against the code that actually exists. Triggers include "/ajian-grill NN", "sharpen work order N", "grill the next feature", "recon before building". This is grill-2, the fact-heavy micro pass: it recons the real code, asks only the genuine decisions the work order left open, gathers the design brief for a UI feature, and promotes the work order from `Depth: brief` to `Depth: detailed` — deeper on WHAT must be true, never HOW. Stops there: it never writes code, plans, or implementation steps. ([pripanggalih/ajian](https://github.com/pripanggalih/ajian/tree/HEAD/skills/grill) · ★1)
- **ajian-map**: Use to find out where you are in the ajian pipeline and which skill comes next. A state-aware router: it reads the project's artifacts (blueprint? work order Depth? plan? git?) and answers "you are at work order NN, next: &lt;skill&gt;". The only ajian skill with no preconditions, so it is always safe to run — reach for it afte… ([pripanggalih/ajian](https://github.com/pripanggalih/ajian/tree/HEAD/skills/map) · ★1)
- **ajian-plan**: Use to turn one detailed work order into a bite-sized implementation plan, before touching code. Triggers include "/ajian-plan NN", "plan work order N", "write the implementation plan". Reads a `Depth: detailed` work order and writes the plan the executor follows; its checkboxes become the build ledger. Stops at the plan — ajian-build executes it. - **ajian-review**: Use to review a finished build and integrate the branch. Triggers include "/ajian-review NN", "review this branch", "review work order N", "finish and merge". - **ajps-data-analysis**: Use when executing and reporting the analysis for an American Journal of Political Science (AJPS) manuscript. Guides analysis and reporting norms; it does not fabricate results. - **ajps-literature-positioning**: Use when positioning an American Journal of Political Science (AJPS) manuscript against the literature. Stakes the contribution; it does not write the lit review. - **ajps-rebuttal**: Use when writing the response to an American Journal of Political Science (AJPS) revise-and-resubmit. Structures the response; it does not fabricate new results. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/American-Journal-of-Political-Science-Skills/skills/ajps-rebuttal) · ★1.1k)
- **ajps-replication-and-verification**: Use when building the replication package for an American Journal of Political Science (AJPS) manuscript. AJPS is famous for MANDATORY third-party PRE-publication verification — an independent verifier re-runs your deposited code and confirms it reproduces the numerical results in the main text before the article is published, with materials deposited to the AJPS Dataverse on Harvard Dataverse. Prepares the package; it does not waive requirements. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/American-Journal-of-Political-Science-Skills/skills/ajps-replication-and-verification) · ★1.1k)
- **ajps-research-design**: Use when defending the research design of an American Journal of Political Science (AJPS) manuscript — causal identification for observational work, experimental and survey-experimental design, formal-empirical linkage, or case-based inference. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/American-Journal-of-Political-Science-Skills/skills/ajps-research-design) · ★1.1k)
- **ajps-review-process**: Use to understand how the American Journal of Political Science (AJPS) evaluates a manuscript — double-blind peer review, desk screening, decision categories, the methodology-only Research Note and Correspondence routes, and the third-party verification that follows acceptance. - **ajps-submission**: Use when running the final pre-submission preflight for the American Journal of Political Science (AJPS) via Editorial Manager — submission-type selection, full anonymizing for double-blind review, word/abstract caps, APSA-or-Chicago formatting, the 25-page Supporting Information limit, and human-subjects/IRB documentation. - **ajps-tables-figures**: Use when building tables and figures for an American Journal of Political Science (AJPS) manuscript. - **ajps-theory-building**: Use when building the theoretical argument of an American Journal of Political Science (AJPS) manuscript — whether empirical with explicit mechanisms, formal/game-theoretic, or measurement-driven. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/American-Journal-of-Political-Science-Skills/skills/ajps-theory-building) · ★1.1k)
- **ajps-topic-selection**: Use when deciding whether a political-science project fits the American Journal of Political Science (AJPS) and which submission type (Article, Research Note, Correspondence) to target. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/American-Journal-of-Political-Science-Skills/skills/ajps-topic-selection) · ★1.1k)
- **ajps-workflow**: Use as the entry point for any American Journal of Political Science (AJPS) manuscript. Routes to the right AJPS sub-skill based on lifecycle stage and which submission type (Article, Research Note, Correspondence) fits, and reminds you that AJPS re-runs your code via a third-party verifier before publishing. - **ajps-writing-style**: Use when drafting or polishing an American Journal of Political Science (AJPS) manuscript so it fits the word caps (Articles &lt;= 10,000 words; Research Notes and Correspondence &lt;= 4,000; abstract &lt;= 150 words), follows the APSA Style Manual or Chicago 18th edition, and stays fully anonymized for double-blind review. Tightens prose and format; it does not invent content. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/American-Journal-of-Political-Science-Skills/skills/ajps-writing-style) · ★1.1k)
- **ajs-data-analysis**: Use when planning or auditing the analysis of an American Journal of Sociology (AJS) manuscript so the evidence credibly supports the theoretical claim. Covers analysis norms, uncertainty, robustness, and triangulation across quantitative, comparative-historical, and ethnographic work. Improves the analysis chain; it does not fabricate results. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/American-Journal-of-Sociology-Skills/skills/ajs-data-analysis) · ★1.1k)
- **ajs-data-and-transparency**: Use when handling data documentation, sharing, and confidentiality for an American Journal of Sociology (AJS) manuscript. Prepares documentation; it does not over-state requirements. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/American-Journal-of-Sociology-Skills/skills/ajs-data-and-transparency) · ★1.1k)
- **ajs-literature-positioning**: Use when positioning an American Journal of Sociology (AJS) manuscript within the literature so it reads as in dialogue with current sociology. AJS reviewers are assigned by readers who locate the paper's substantive area and method, and the journal prizes engagement with live theoretical debates. Maps the conversation; it does not write the literature review. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/American-Journal-of-Sociology-Skills/skills/ajs-literature-positioning) · ★1.1k)
- **ajs-rebuttal**: Use when responding to an American Journal of Sociology (AJS) decision — an R&R response letter to multiple double-blind reviewers and the editor, or an author Reply to a published Comment in AJS's Comment-and-Reply tradition. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/American-Journal-of-Sociology-Skills/skills/ajs-rebuttal) · ★1.1k)
